### sqlite-071 — Move, share or reference data instead of copying it
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite · Also: PostgreSQL
Principle: When the source no longer needs a buffer, transfer the pointer. When the consumer reads data right away, hand it a pointer into the existing buffer. When several consumers need the same large immutable value, share it by reference count. Track properties such as "NUL-terminated" so no one copies to establish them.
Why it is faster: A copy costs an allocation, a memcpy and a later free, proportional to the data size. Moving a pointer is constant time. The risk is lifetime: an ephemeral pointer is valid only until the source moves, and a shared value must be immutable.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Transferable `ArrayBuffer`s in `postMessage`; `Buffer.subarray` instead of copies in Node; share request bodies between middleware stages without cloning. • Native / systems: C++ move semantics, Rust ownership and `Arc<str>`; `string_view`/`&str` parsing; C APIs that take a free callback. • Data / databases: Zero-copy row views in drivers; decode a column only when it is read. • Embedded: Pass buffer ownership through a queue instead of copying payloads between tasks.
Watch out: Ephemeral pointers dangle if anything moves the source in between; restrict them to paths where nothing can. Reference counts need atomic updates if shared across threads.

### sqlite-072 — Pass values in the form the consumer needs and write results straight into their destination
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite · Also: PostgreSQL
Principle: Do not serialize data only for the callee to parse it back; pass the decoded values. Do not have a producer write into a temporary that the caller copies; give the producer a pointer to the final slot.
Why it is faster: A serialize-deserialize round trip inside one process costs encoding, allocation and decoding with nothing gained. An intermediate temporary costs a copy and the code that manages it. Both disappear when the interface accepts the in-memory form and an output location.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Do not JSON-encode between modules in the same process; pass objects. • Native / systems: Return-value optimization and emplacement; decoders that write into a caller-provided buffer. • Data / databases: Pass parsed tuples between operators, not serialized rows; point executor operators at their parent's output slots. • Embedded: DMA or decode straight into the final structure instead of a staging buffer.
Watch out: Writing straight into the destination means a failure halfway leaves it partly written; make sure callers tolerate that or write only after validation.
Seen in SQLite: e14006d0de 2008-03-25 `IdxDelete` takes a register array, which "avoids a needless packing and unpacking of the record". 8cff69df11 2009-11-12 lets `OP_Found`/`OP_NotFound` take unpacked registers to avoid building records during IN processing. • 808ec7c0c7 2008-07-29 `OP_Column` decodes directly into the destination register instead of through a temporary. • 05a86c5c0f 2014-02-16 INSERT...SELECT puts SELECT output straight into the INSERT's registers, avoiding copies. • 9bd038f12e 2014-08-27 `sqlite3_context` holds `pOut`, a pointer to the result register, instead of an embedded value copied back with memcpy: "runs faster and saves over 500 bytes of code space". • 4b727f17d6 2016-11-11 `OP_IdxInsert` takes unpacked key registers so no serialized record is built: speedtest1 2.6% faster. • Also seen in PostgreSQL (postgresql-093): 9f03ca91519 2014-07-01 Forms index tuples directly in the sort context, which "shaves several percent off the index build time". • 2f48ede080f 2020-06-12 RETURN QUERY pushes tuples straight into the tuplestore instead of through a cursor. • 17a5871d9d5 2024-07-27 JSON escaping takes text and length directly, so no NUL-terminated temporary copy is needed. • b61aa76e458 2025-12-07 `jsonb_agg` builds its tree directly in the aggregate context, about 2x faster on text input. 005a2907dc3 2025-12-07 converts ints and numerics to jsonb directly instead of via text: +30% for integers, nearly 2x for numeric. • 257c8231bf9 2026-04-07 `pg_buffercache_pages()` builds the result tuplestore directly instead of a custom array.

### sqlite-073 — Size allocations from actual need, and use all of what the allocator returns
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite
Principle: Compute the real element count before allocating rather than the theoretical maximum, allocate only for the part of the input that will be processed, and defer an allocation until its size is known. When the allocator rounds up, query the usable size and use the slack, including for secondary arrays.
Why it is faster: Worst-case sizing wastes memory and cache; a tight estimate also avoids later shrinking and memmove. Allocators round requests up to size classes, so the tail of a block is free capacity: using it means fewer reallocations, and placing auxiliary arrays there can save a whole allocation.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Size response buffers from Content-Length; reserve builders from a cheap pre-scan. • Native / systems: `malloc_usable_size`/`_msize` in vectors; arena allocators that return the true block size; handles issued as counters with tables allocated at flush time. • Data / databases: Size operator memory from cardinality estimates; skip memory for already-processed ranges in incremental jobs. • Embedded: Compute exact buffer sizes at configuration time instead of reserving maximums.
Watch out: Underestimates turn into reallocations; keep the estimate conservative where growth is expensive. Usable-size queries are allocator-specific and must be abstracted.
Seen in SQLite: 634f298c89 2005-03-28 `balance_nonroot` sums real sibling cell counts instead of allocating for the maximum. e3dd8bb5be 2006-02-27 cut a Windows driver allocation "to the minimum needed", and 2e6400ba9e 2007-05-08 cut surplus memory in `replace()`. • 6a1e071f3d 2008-12-05 Uses `sqlite3DbMallocSize` "to maximize the size of growable buffers after each reallocation". c702c7ccdd 2008-07-18 makes memory statistics use the real size. • b2771ce24c 2009-02-20 Reuses "left-over opcode space at the end of the VDBE opcode array to store memory cells, VDBE cursors, and other content", which reduces prepared-statement memory. 19875c8240 2009-12-08 and 4800b2ee95 2009-12-08 document the two-pass scheme: fit everything in the slack first, then allocate once for the rest. cd3e8f7ce9 2008-03-25 had earlier carved cursor space from a memory cell. • 7f5a7ecd21 2014-11-03 The string accumulator records the allocator's full usable size, not the requested size. • bd57308eef 2016-01-01 Stores the byte size of the opcode array so no `sqlite3_msize()` call is needed. • ec4ccdbcb1 2018-12-29 Making a label became `return ADDR(pParse->nLabel++)`; the label table is allocated only when a label is first resolved, when its size is nearly known: "faster and makes fewer memory allocations". • 2ff73a5f3e 2023-12-04 The JSON parser reserves `(nJson-i)` bytes for an object header instead of twice that, giving "fewer reallocations and memmove operations". 611b9d3efd 2023-12-19 sizes `WhereInfo` exactly for single-table queries. • 09a5513008 2026-09-01 A WAL checkpoint allocates no index memory for hash pages that are already backfilled.

### sqlite-074 — Use an inline or stack buffer for the common size, with heap fallback
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite · Also: PostgreSQL
Principle: Embed a small fixed buffer in the object, or put per-call objects of known size on the stack, sized for most inputs. Go to the heap only when the input is larger.
Why it is faster: Stack and inline storage are a pointer bump with nothing to free and no out-of-memory path. Most strings, keys and parser stacks are small, so most calls never touch the allocator. Growing the inline buffer a little can cover more of the distribution.
Applies elsewhere: Native / systems: Small-string optimization, `SmallVec`, LLVM `SmallVector`; fixed parser state on the stack. • Web / backend: In Go, keep short-lived values from escaping to the heap. • Embedded: Fixed-depth parser stacks with heap spill instead of a hard limit. • Data / databases: Inline key buffers for comparisons, with heap fallback for long keys.
Watch out: Large stack buffers break small-stack targets (threads, embedded tasks); see the next entry. Size the inline buffer from measurements, not guesses.
Seen in SQLite: 371ac44d1c 2003-01-07 Used "static string space rather than mallocing (when possible) for a small speed improvement"; it became the `Mem.zShort` buffer. • b41392241e 2013-11-06 Grew the `btreeMoveto` stack buffer from 150 to 200 bytes and the `OP_Found` temporary from 3 to 4 Mems, "reducing the need to malloc". • c6aab32144 2016-01-23 spellfix uses stack space for the edit-distance matrix where that makes sense. • cb43a937e5 2016-10-03 The Parse object became a local in `sqlite3Prepare()` instead of a stack-or-heap allocation: "substantial performance increase and a size reduction". The same commit grew the `btreeMoveto` buffer to 384 bytes. • d26cc54183 2017-01-28 In the amalgamation the parser engine lives in a stack buffer instead of being allocated. 6903bf6d49 2017-08-01 put Parse objects on the stack in more places: "slightly smaller and faster". • ad2689fe4e 2024-01-27 The parser stack starts in an inline array and grows on the heap, which removed the fixed-depth "parser stack overflow" error. • Also seen in PostgreSQL (postgresql-094): 8fbef3b55dd 2002-11-17 `varstr_cmp()` uses two 1024-byte stack buffers and pallocs only for longer strings. • d961a568996 2005-06-19 `_bt_first` uses a fixed local scan-key array instead of palloc. 0d9742f99aa 2006-03-01 StringInfo on the stack where it doesn't outlive the frame. • a63bbc811d4 2026-04-13 Stack `StringInfoData` instead of `makeStringInfo()`. d071e1cfec2 2026-03-12 B-tree scans no longer allocate a descent stack (only inserts need it), reducing churn for skip scans.

### sqlite-075 — Bound stack usage: compute the maximum, move big locals out, iterate instead of recurse
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite
Principle: Know and limit how much stack the code needs. Compute exact worst-case depths statically where possible, move large locals to the heap on small-stack builds, keep locals in narrow scopes, and turn deep recursion into loops with explicit work lists.
Why it is faster: A bounded footprint lets the code run on threads and embedded tasks with small stacks, and a smaller frame is cheaper to set up on every call. Static analysis allocates exactly what a program needs instead of a generous fixed amount. Iteration removes stack-overflow risk on deep or adversarial input and often lets the compiler inline.
Applies elsewhere: Embedded: Budget RTOS task stacks from measured frame sizes (`-fstack-usage`); no large local arrays. • Native / systems: Iterative destructors for long lists and trees in C++/Rust to avoid overflow on drop; bottom-up merge sort in kernels. • Web / backend: Iterative tree walks in JS to avoid stack overflow on deep JSON or ASTs. • Data / databases: Compute maximum operator stack depth when compiling a plan, as the JVM does with `max_stack`.
Watch out: Moving buffers to the heap trades stack safety for allocator calls; make it a build option where speed matters more.
Seen in SQLite: 2e38c32b67 2004-09-03 Moved large B-tree structures from the stack to malloc for "embedded systems and pthreads implementations that only allocate a limited amount of stack space". 79158e1865 2005-09-06 reduced stack use generally. • bc04f8529d 2005-03-29 Computes the VM's exact worst-case stack depth from per-opcode bitmasks generated at build time, instead of allocating a generous fixed stack. • e7b3470705 2009-06-01 Mallocs the Parse object (or uses alloca). 50d654da3b 2009-06-03 added `SQLITE_SMALL_STACK` and turned a 500-byte `round()` buffer into a heap string. 6ee700c32c 2009-06-01 removed large stack objects from blob I/O. • a50d9aa784 2009-06-08 Made B-tree balancing non-recursive. f6963f99dd 2009-11-23 made expression deletion take one frame per level instead of two. • f544b4c4b8 2010-06-25 Made the WAL merge sort non-recursive with a fixed array of sublists. 271065704c 2010-12-01 frees trigger frames from a list in a loop instead of recursively. • a2af0aecdb 2012-03-14 FTS stopped allocating a large object on the stack. c65714ddf0 2012-09-18 added a script that measures per-function stack frames from the disassembly. • 6b36e82c7b 2013-07-30 Moved a large `Expr` temporary from the stack to the heap. 2c77be054a 2013-11-27 removed locals from `sqlite3VdbeExec()` "to reduce stack-space requirements". • ebf9702b34 2024-05-06 Expression deletion frees a node and jumps back to handle its left child, so long unary chains use constant stack.

### sqlite-076 — Allocate scratch space once per owner, not once per operation
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite
Principle: Move per-call temporaries to the lifetime of the object that uses them. When an object is almost always needed, create it at open time so each use skips the "created yet?" test; split "allocate" from "fill" so the fixed setup is done once.
Why it is faster: An allocation per comparison, insert or read multiplies allocator cost by the operation count. A buffer owned by the cursor, sorter or connection is allocated once and reused. Eager creation trades a little memory for removing a branch from every hot call.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Reuse `TextDecoder`, buffers and regexes per connection or worker; preallocate per-thread scratch in HTTP servers. • Native / systems: Per-thread scratch buffers in image codecs; avoid `if (!this.x) this.x = ...` in hot getters when `x` is always used. • Data / databases: Per-cursor key buffers; per-operator hash tables reused across batches. • Embedded: Static scratch areas reused by every call into a codec.
Watch out: Owner-scoped buffers are not reentrant; a recursive or concurrent use of the same owner needs its own buffer.
Seen in SQLite: 7f72114547 2011-09-02 Sorter comparisons reuse a sorter-owned buffer for the unpacked record instead of a stack buffer or malloc per compare, to "reduce the number of malloc() calls when creating an index on more than 2 columns". • 42acb3e597 2011-09-05 and 03e9cfc223 2011-09-05 Split unpacked-record allocation from filling: fixed fields are set once, and unpacking only fills them. This fixed "a minor performance regression". • c3031c61ef 2014-08-26 Creates the page cache object when the cache is opened instead of on first fetch: "noticable performance boost". • 3fbb022b98 2014-09-24 Allocates `BtShared.pTmpSpace` when the first write cursor opens instead of on each insert or delete. 2f0bc1d41b 2021-12-03 moved that allocation to an out-of-line helper, making cursor creation about 800K cycles faster. • 48c8405f99 2025-11-23 The JS kvvfs replaced "an alloc/free on every read" of a key with a one-time allocation.

### sqlite-077 — Fit hot objects to the allocator's size classes
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite
Principle: Know the slot sizes of the allocator in use, and shrink objects that land just above a boundary so they fall into the faster or smaller class. Allocate page-sized buffers separately from their metadata so the buffer fills an exact power-of-two slot.
Why it is faster: An object one byte over a slab slot falls back to the slow general allocator or wastes most of a larger slot. Trimming a field can move it back. A page buffer with a header attached becomes a non-power-of-two size, which wastes slab space and hurts alignment.
Applies elsewhere: Native / systems: Keep hot objects under jemalloc/tcmalloc class boundaries; keep a struct within one 64-byte line. • Web / backend: Keep V8 objects under the in-object property limit; keep messages under the MTU. • Data / databases: Buffer pools with separate descriptor arrays so frames are exact page size. • Embedded: Match struct sizes to fixed-block pool sizes.
Watch out: Size classes differ between allocators and platforms; a static assert on `sizeof` catches regressions on the platforms that matter.
Seen in SQLite: ce7e189d24 2010-12-01 Narrowed `SrcList.iSelectId` to `u8` and moved it "so that it fits in a 100 byte lookaside buffer on a 64-bit architecture". • 22e21ff4fc 2011-11-08 The pcache2 interface lets page buffers and their headers be allocated separately (`SQLITE_PCACHE_SEPARATE_HEADER`), so buffers fit exact page-size slots. • cbb9da337e 2019-12-12, c4938ea2ba 2019-12-13 and 85d9411c09 2019-12-13 Cut `ExprList` items from 32 to 24 bytes on 64-bit "to keep lookaside allocations in the mini-size".

### sqlite-078 — Offer a deterministic allocator over a fixed memory region
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite
Principle: Let the embedder hand the library fixed buffers for its heap, page cache and scratch space, managed by an allocator with bounded worst-case behaviour (size-class free lists, a buddy system). Keep the fast path O(1) and the per-block header small.
Why it is faster: On systems without a good malloc, or that forbid one, a fixed pool gives predictable memory and fragmentation. A power-of-two buddy allocator finds and frees blocks in constant or logarithmic time. Taking the first free block of the right class is O(1); searching for the lowest address costs time and buys little.
Applies elsewhere: Embedded: Buddy or size-class allocators on bare metal and RTOS; no system malloc after boot. • Native / systems: Per-frame linear allocators in games; boundary-tag packing in custom mallocs. • Data / databases: Carve the buffer pool from one large mapping. • Web / backend: Fixed-size arenas for latency-critical services that cannot tolerate allocator stalls.
Watch out: Custom allocators age: once the system allocator catches up, a special pool is just extra code and branches, as SQLite found with its scratch allocator.
Seen in SQLite: 9c7a60dfb2 2007-10-19 A malloc-free allocator over one static array, with exact-size free lists, a hashed list and a bump-allocated master chunk: "Runs 7% faster than when using system malloc() on Linux". 71f971b2da 2007-12-29 shrank the per-allocation overhead by letting the previous-size field overlap the previous chunk's data while that chunk is in use. • 2d7636e212 2008-02-16 A power-of-two first-fit (buddy) allocator over a static pool. 5099be5e85 2008-06-27 made the minimum block size configurable. • 335897970c 2008-06-18 Added configuration for static scratch, page-cache and heap buffers; facf030792 2008-06-17 added the internal APIs that use them. • da17af08fd 2008-07-25 Stores the offset from pointer to chunk in a header so free is fast. • bdf1e2437e 2010-06-25 Moved the checkpoint iterator's large buffer to scratch memory and made it smaller; badc980afa 2010-08-27 refactored the scratch allocator. • 9948e95674 2013-11-23 The buddy allocator takes "the first on the list of free blocks rather than searching for the one with the smallest address. This is also faster than using the min-heap algorithm." • b2a0f75c06 2017-08-28 Removed the scratch allocator once it served only niche configurations, replacing it with a hint flag: "smaller, faster, and easier to maintain". • f5a8386c32 2022-03-28 Buddy size rounding handles the two smallest sizes with direct compares and steps the doubling loop by 4x with one halving check.

### sqlite-079 — Allocate uniform nodes in large chunks and free them all at once
Lens: allocation · Source: SQLite
Principle: For many small entries of one size (set elements, cache pages), allocate blocks of many entries at a time and release the whole block together, instead of one malloc per entry. Defer the block until first use and cap it at the configured limit.
Why it is faster: One allocation per node pays allocator time and header overhead per insert and scatters nodes across the heap. A chunk amortizes the call over many entries and packs them together. Freeing everything at once makes teardown O(chunks).
Applies elsewhere: Native / systems: Arena-backed trees and linked lists; Go `[]Node` storage with index links instead of pointers. • Web / backend: Object pools for network buffers allocated in batches. • Data / databases: Slab-allocated hash-table entries and cache frames. • Embedded: Preallocated connection or message structs in one array.
Watch out: An eager chunk wastes memory if few entries are used; defer it and size it by measurement.
Seen in SQLite: 3d4501e573 2008-12-04 RowSet replaced a FIFO, allocating many entries per chunk "to reduce the malloc overhead". 733bf1b1e2 2009-04-22 extended it with a 1024-byte chunk target; it "uses less memory" than the hash it replaced. • ee70a84ea7 2015-07-06 and 104a25a034 2015-07-08 Each page cache preallocates 100 pages in one block, with the slots on a free list: "5.2% performance improvement". • 957026ac78 2015-07-16 Defers that block until the first page request, caps it at the cache size, and frees it under memory pressure if unused. • 83a4f47d57 2017-01-02 Reduced the default preallocation from 100 to 20 pages, "experimentally determined to be slightly faster".

### sqlite-080 — Cache the decoded form next to the raw data, and decode only as far as needed
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite
Principle: When the same encoded record or page is read repeatedly, keep its parsed header and offsets with the cursor or cached page, decode lazily up to the furthest field requested, and compare or read in place when the data is fully local.
Why it is faster: Reading column 5 of a record with a variable-width header means parsing the offsets of columns 0 to 4. Without a cache, each column read repeats that parse. With one, later reads are a table lookup. Stopping at the highest column used avoids parsing columns no one asks for, and requesting the rightmost column first fills the whole offset cache in one pass.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Lazy row objects in DB drivers that decode a column on first access and remember it; partial JSON decoding that stops at the needed field. • Data / databases: Cache per-page slot directories and per-row offset tables; compare keys in place in mmap'd indexes. • Native / systems: Precomputed sort keys (Schwartzian transform) so comparisons do not re-parse; per-row parse caches in CSV readers. • Embedded: Cache the parsed header of the last-read record from flash.
Watch out: The cache must be invalidated whenever the cursor moves or the page changes; every such path needs a test.
Seen in SQLite: 9188b38919 2004-05-14 Cached record headers in `OP_Column`. e61cffc203 2004-06-12 also keeps a pointer to the whole row when it sits on one page. • 9792eeff95 2006-01-13 Computes the highest column a query uses from a bitmask and stops header parsing there: "Avoid parsing an entire record header when it is not required". • eaa06f6970 2008-09-18 Keeps an "initialized" state on cached B-tree pages so a cache hit skips re-parsing the page structure. 897a820f46 2008-09-18 skips the page-number bound check when the page is already in cache. • 11c327aabe 2009-05-04 Compares index keys directly against cell bytes on the page when the cell fits entirely locally, instead of copying them out. • 8b1ea14f8c 2011-09-03 The sorter merge keeps the unchanged side of a comparison unpacked instead of re-decoding it for every compare. • 9f8952390a 2018-01-24 Sorter output columns are read right to left, so the first read parses the full header and later reads of columns to its left hit the cached offsets.

### sqlite-081 — Store a hot derived value in the object, recomputed only when its inputs change
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite · Also: ares
Principle: If the hot path computes the same value from slowly changing inputs (a threshold from configuration, a pointer from base plus offset, the current element of an array), compute it when the inputs change and store it in its own field.
Why it is faster: The hot path replaces arithmetic and dependent loads (`p->a[p->i]`, `p->aMem[0].z`) with one load. Inputs such as page size or configuration change rarely, so keeping the copy in sync costs almost nothing. The trade-off is one more field to update on every cold transition.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Precompute eviction watermarks when an LRU's capacity changes; cache resolved URLs and paths in connection objects. • Native / systems: Keep a "current leaf" pointer in tree iterators; store `data_start` in packet structs; cache `stride*y` per image row. • Data / databases: Remember the last allocated ID in memory instead of querying the max key. • Embedded: Derive masks and shifts from configuration once at init.
Watch out: A stored derived value that is not refreshed on some rare transition is a silent bug; assert it against the recomputed value in debug builds.
Seen in SQLite: 32fbe342db 2002-10-19 The cursor remembers the next rowid to allocate, so an insert does not seek to the end of the tree to find it. • 3e875ef3b5 2010-07-05 The WAL file name is built once when the pager is created, not on every read transaction. • 41692e9d7c 2011-01-25 The page cache stores its pinned-page limit and its 90% purge threshold, recomputed only on resize, create or destroy; part of "shave a few cycles so that performance is better than 3.7.4". • f44890a7c4 2015-06-27 Added `MemPage.aDataOfst = aData + childPtrSize`, set at page init, used in the B-tree binary search: "about a 2 million cycle gain". • 75e96b3100 2017-04-01 Added `BtCursor.ix` instead of `aiIdx[iPage]`: "Save 78 bytes of code space and a million CPU cycles". 352a35abf5 2017-08-15 added `BtCursor.pPage`, "saving a single pointer dereference on each access". • b6991796b4 2018-12-28 Moved the opcode array capacity into the VM object "to avoid an extra pointer deference on the fast path". • 2ef111168f 2022-02-06 Computes an expression's height from its children's cached heights instead of walking the subtree. • 584bfcaeb6 2022-02-23 Stores the page number of the lock page instead of recomputing `PENDING_BYTE/pageSize+1`: "It turns out that number is needed quite frequently." • f357cafaf5 2022-02-27 Copies the first key field's value and length into `UnpackedRecord`, which "avoids extra indirections and saves about 750K cycles" in comparisons. • Also seen in ares (ares-017): `PPU::interlace()/overscan()/vdisp()` used to test `system.fastPPU()` and forward to the other PPU or compute `!io.overscan ? 225 : 240`. They now return `display.interlace`, `display.overscan` and `display.vdisp`, cached fields (277→291 fps with the fast PPU). The fast PPU also renders mode 7 and sprites from a local cached `cgram[]` instead of `ppu.cgram`. 5b97fa241 2018-06 “Update to v106r42 release.” · 5d29700fa 2018-05 “Update to v106r33 release.”

### sqlite-082 — Compute an expensive per-item value on first use and cache it behind a sentinel
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite · Also: Netty
Principle: Mark a derived value "unknown" (0 or -1) when the object is created, compute it the first time someone asks, and store it. Keep the "already cached?" test inline and put the computation in an out-of-line function.
Why it is faster: Many derived values (a cell's size, a page's free space, an index's affinity string) are needed for some objects and never for others. Eager computation pays for all of them; lazy memoization pays once, only for the ones used. Keeping the slow path out of line keeps the check small enough to inline.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Lazy getters on JS classes; memoized computed properties on cached schema metadata. • Native / systems: Rust `OnceCell`; Java's lazily computed `String.hashCode`. • Data / databases: Lazy column statistics; per-page summaries computed on first write. • Embedded: Compute lookup tables on first use when flash is too small to store them.
Watch out: The sentinel must never be a legal value, and every mutation must reset it. If nearly every object ends up needing the value, eager computation is simpler and avoids the check.
Seen in SQLite: 1ffd247c0c 2015-06-23 B-tree balancing keeps a side array of cell sizes and computes each on first request: "overall 1.7% performance gain for about 1000 extra bytes". 008d64c23a 2015-06-23 and f783893b8d 2015-06-23 carried it into other phases, and 9c739e26da 2015-06-24 reached "2.6% faster overall with a size increase of less than 750 bytes". • b0ea9432a1 2019-02-09 Page free space starts at -1 and is computed when needed, "since for many pages it is never needed"; 3e7800a9db 2019-02-12 finished the change. • ca4cf12d70 2023-02-27 `sqlite3IndexAffinityStr()` checks the cached string inline and calls an out-of-line builder only on a miss. • Also seen in Netty (netty-076): 0052d83db1 2013-12-18 `HttpObjectDecoder.contentLength()` is cached with a `Long.MIN_VALUE` sentinel and reset per message. • 25f96b1644 2013-07-22 `DefaultChannelPromise` records that it has been validated, reducing write overhead from promise checks.

### sqlite-083 — Maintain cheap summary flags so the common "nothing to do" case skips work
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite
Principle: Keep a precomputed bit or count that answers "does any item of this kind exist?" (listeners, special cursors, NOT NULL columns, shared caches, hooks), updated where items are added or the configuration changes, and test it before scanning lists or calling subsystems.
Why it is faster: Most of the time the answer is "none", and one bit test replaces a list walk or a chain of conditions. Maintaining the flag costs a store on rare events. An aggregate (the sum of reference counts) is cheaper to maintain than tracking which items cross zero.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: A `hasListeners` flag before building event objects; skip middleware when no hooks are registered. • Native / systems: A "has dirty children" bit in UI and scene trees; one combined pending-work mask checked on each loop iteration. • Data / databases: Table-level flags (has triggers, has foreign keys, has generated columns) checked before per-row machinery. • Embedded: One event-pending word polled in the main loop.
Watch out: A flag that is set but never cleared degrades to "always do the work", and one that is cleared too eagerly skips required work; recompute it during the scan it guards.
Seen in SQLite: 5d56dd2867 2013-12-13 Added an explicit `isPinned` flag instead of inferring list membership from neighbour pointers, so the pin routine is "called much less often and runs much faster". • 6918095d81 2015-06-25 `Btree.hasIncrblobCur` lets the incremental-blob invalidation return immediately in the usual case. • 27fb746cde 2015-06-30 A `BTCF_Multiple` flag is set only when two cursors share a root, so saving cursors runs only then. • 95a0b37156 2015-09-03 The page cache tracks the sum of all reference counts instead of the number of referenced pages, "for a performance improvement and size reduction". • 04c6747a80 2018-12-04 Installing a legacy profile callback also sets a bit in the trace mask, so `sqlite3_step` tests one mask instead of two conditions. • cbda9c7ac7 2019-10-26 A `TF_HasNotNull` table flag lets constraint generation skip the per-column NOT NULL loop entirely. • aa39569616 2026-10-15 Enabled the "no shared cache" fast path in single-threaded builds too, so per-statement shared-cache checks are skipped.

### sqlite-084 — Keep a small bounded cache of parsed or compiled objects, keyed by input identity
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite
Principle: When the same input is parsed, compiled or opened repeatedly, keep the last few results in a tiny cache with LRU replacement. Match by pointer identity first and by length plus content second. Reuse handles that are already open on the same object.
Why it is faster: Parsing or compiling dominates when the same text recurs (the same JSON argument, the same SQL). A one-slot cache thrashes when two inputs alternate; four slots usually hold the working set. Comparing pointers first makes most hits nearly free.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Prepared-statement caches in DB drivers; compiled template and regex caches; per-request caches of decoded JWTs. • Native / systems: Shader and pipeline caches; DNS answer caches per request. • Data / databases: Plan caches keyed by normalized SQL; handle caches for files and cursors. • Embedded: A two- or four-entry cache of the last decoded records.
Watch out: Measure the hit rate; SQLite's own commit admits a first version that barely helped. Pointer-identity keys are only valid while the source buffer is alive and unchanged.
Seen in SQLite: fb7e7651ca 2005-01-24 The Tcl interface keeps an LRU list of prepared statements to "cache VMs and reuse them without recompiling". • e35fc302c3 2018-08-30 The JSON parse cache grew from one slot to four, with hold counters: a hit is promoted and the least recently held entry is evicted. • ca1ce7773c 2023-12-01, 5bfa7e65d1 2023-12-01 and 063d0d4c3a 2023-12-01 Reworked the JSON cache on the statement context; the first message admits "the cache seems not to help much", and later work made it "more effective". 03075fa868 2023-12-01 scans for a matching pointer first, then compares length and bytes, and moves a hit to the most recently used end. • 21d4f0cc7b 2023-09-02 Client-data lookup tests pointer equality before `strcmp`. • 5e71497404 2025-07-01 `OP_VOpen` reuses a virtual-table cursor that is already open on the same table instead of closing and reopening it.

### sqlite-085 — Cache large reassembled values, with invalidation scoped as narrowly as possible
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite
Principle: When an expensive-to-build value (a large blob gathered from many pages) is often read more than once, cache the last one keyed by its location, only above a size threshold, and restrict the cache to cases where few operations can invalidate it.
Why it is faster: Reassembling a multi-page value costs I/O and copying; a repeated read of the same column within a row then costs nothing. The threshold keeps small values, which are cheap to re-read, from paying the bookkeeping. Narrow scope keeps unrelated writes from having to invalidate the cache.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Memoize parsed JSON blobs per row in an ORM. • Data / databases: Cache decompressed chunks in columnar readers, keyed by file and offset. • Native / systems: Cache decoded images or assets keyed by location.
Watch out: Large cached values pin memory; keep the cache to one or a few entries and drop it with the cursor.
Seen in SQLite: 594f5e24c4 2023-07-27 Values longer than 4000 bytes are cached per cursor, keyed by column and B-tree offset, and returned as shared reference-counted strings; the comment notes the overhead only pays for large values. • 859dd4fc2f 2023-07-28 JSON functions use this cache. • 00d635dda4 2023-07-31 Restricted the cache to table B-trees, so index inserts and deletes no longer bump the invalidation counter.

### sqlite-086 — Guess with a tiny hint, verify cheaply, fall back to the full search
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite
Principle: Put a very small hint structure in front of an expensive lookup: a cached list of positions from an earlier walk, a likely next position, or a 16-entry hash-indexed array of likely slots. Use the hint if it checks out, and search normally if it does not.
Why it is faster: The hint is right most of the time, turning an O(n) scan or a chain walk into O(1). Verification is cheap (one compare or a small metadata read), and a wrong hint costs only that check. Memory cost is a few bytes.
Applies elsewhere: Native / systems: Shape and slot hints in JS engines; extent caches for random access into large files. • Web / backend: Small per-object property caches; route caches in front of a trie router. • Data / databases: Prefetch guesses validated against a compact metadata index. • Embedded: Remember the last matched table entry before scanning.
Watch out: The hint must be verified every time; an unverified hint is a correctness bug, not a performance trick.
Seen in SQLite: 2dec97077b 2007-05-02 Incremental blob I/O caches the overflow page numbers of a value after the first walk. • d04417963f 2007-05-02 On a miss it guesses the next overflow page is the current one plus one and checks the guess in the small pointer-map page instead of reading the full page. • 66172ceb89 2025-02-08 Added a 16-byte `Table.aHx` array mapping a column-name hash to a likely column index, tried before the full scan. 9d90a3af2f 2025-02-08 routed more lookups through it, and 3bdebaeabb 2025-02-09 tightened the loop: "Performance and size optimization".

### sqlite-087 — Validate a cache with a cheap version stamp instead of discarding it
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite · Also: PostgreSQL
Principle: Attach a change counter or generation number to cached data. On reuse, compare one number to decide whether the cache is still good. To reset many entries at once, bump a generation counter instead of clearing each entry.
Why it is faster: Throwing a cache away on every lock release or run costs a full reload even when nothing changed. A counter comparison costs one read. Generation stamps make a reset O(1) instead of O(entries): an entry is valid only if its stamp equals the current generation.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: HTTP ETag / If-None-Match; epoch counters on read-mostly configuration caches. • Native / systems: Frame-number stamps instead of clearing visited flags in game engines and graph searches. • Data / databases: Version stamps on shared-memory catalog caches. • Embedded: A generation byte per cached sensor reading.
Watch out: Counters wrap; reset all stamps when the counter nears its limit, as `OP_Once` does.
Seen in SQLite: e277be0545 2007-03-23 Discards the page cache "only when the change-counter indicates that it is stale". e4dd73b4fa 2007-03-26 skips the unlock and relock cycle entirely in exclusive mode. • 9e5eb9c8cc 2016-09-18 `OP_Once` compares its operand with a counter on `OP_Init` that increments on each run, replacing a flag array and its memset: "smaller, faster, and uses less memory". • Also seen in PostgreSQL (postgresql-107): 293e24e5078 2017-02-07 The hash index metapage is cached in local memory; staleness is detected through a field on the bucket page, which is locked anyway. This avoids a "very significant amount" of buffer traffic. • e369f370863 2020-01-06 WAL senders cache the flush position and refresh it from shared memory only after catching up, which cuts spinlock contention. • 8f59f6b9c03 2020-03-26 A search_path change counter makes revalidating cached simple-expression plans a compare: about 2x for trivial plpgsql expressions. • 623a9ba79bb 2020-08-17 `xactCompletionCount` lets an unchanged snapshot be reused, another 1.03x on a smaller machine and about twice that gain on a larger one.

### sqlite-088 — Invalidate narrowly, and not at all when nothing relevant changed
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite · Also: PostgreSQL
Principle: Scope invalidation to the data actually affected, and skip it when an update provably leaves cached results valid (the value is unchanged, or the change cannot affect the cached result).
Why it is faster: A broad invalidation forces costly rebuilds (re-parsing a schema, re-planning a statement) for data that did not change. Narrow keys and equality checks keep the cache warm. The trade-off is more bookkeeping to know what depends on what.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Bust only the changed bundles in a frontend build cache; fine-grained cache keys instead of a global flush. • Data / databases: Reload ORM metadata only for the altered table; plan caches bucketed by parameter class. • Native / systems: JIT specializations invalidated only when the guarded value class changes. • Embedded: Recompute derived configuration only for the parameters that changed.
Watch out: Every missed dependency is a stale-cache bug; when in doubt, invalidate.
Seen in SQLite: 6498f0bb2e 2010-04-09 Avoids stepping null statements while parsing the schema. • c7792fa052 2011-04-02 Reduced "the scope and frequency of invalidating schemas", resetting only the affected database. 7af72cf963 2011-05-05 skips the schema reset when re-registering a module that has not changed. • c60dd4af07 2026-08-18 Statements whose plan depends on a bound parameter re-plan only when the value moves into or out of the special {0,1} class. 6f1110ce05 2026-08-21 gave `sqlite3_bind_int64()` a specialized path for this. • 0eaef28cf2 2026-08-22 and e037ddd700 2026-10-12 Binding never triggers a re-prepare "if the value does not actually change". • Also seen in PostgreSQL (postgresql-106): 2c5aa2acb49 2001-06-18 A relcache flush removes only matching catcache entries instead of "zapping the caches completely". • 50ee1c7462d 2017-05-12 Catcache invalidation finds the cache by ID directly: 2x on a logical-decoding workload. 2df5d465558 2017-05-12 keeps syscache callbacks in per-cache-ID chains. • 40064a8ee1b 2024-08-07 `hash_seq_init_with_hash_value` iterates only the matching bucket for the attribute-option and type caches. b85a9d046ef 2024-10-24 adds a relid-to-type reverse map for the type cache. • 588acf6d0ec 2025-03-06 and 3abe9dc1889 2025-03-13 invalidate only the affected replication sync entries on publication DDL. 0962f9e3443 2026-07-27 uses a direct hash lookup instead of a full scan in a partition-map callback. • 05172f1f374 2023-02-23 Registers cache callbacks once per session rather than on every startup cycle, which had exhausted slots.

### sqlite-089 — Delete caches whose upkeep costs more than recomputation
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite
Principle: Every cache taxes the writers that keep it valid and adds code and bugs. Measure its net effect, and remove it when hits are rare, when recomputation has become cheap, or when it causes races.
Why it is faster: A cache saves time only if hits times saved cost exceeds the maintenance on every write. As other code gets faster, an old cache can become a net loss. Removing it saves the maintenance, the memory and the code size at once.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Audit `useMemo` on cheap values and per-request caches with low hit rates; remove application caches in front of a database that is now fast enough. • Native / systems: Re-benchmark old object pools and memo tables on current runtimes. • Data / databases: Drop derived-count caches that every write must update. • Embedded: Reclaim RAM from caches that measurements show rarely hit.
Watch out: Measure on representative workloads before deleting; a cache that looks useless on a benchmark can matter for a specific access pattern.
Seen in SQLite: 2ec2fb2269 2013-11-06 Added a KeyInfo cache; 2ea0bafae2 2014-12-12 and 18b67f3f0d 2014-12-12 removed it a year later because it gave "minimal performance improvements, and then only at SQL preparation time" and caused data-race problems. • e0670b6220 2014-02-12 Removed the max-rowid cache: "ineffective. Removing it results in a performance increase of 0.4%, less memory usage, and a slightly smaller library". • b53a5a9e50 2014-10-12 Removed the per-cursor last-rowid cache because "maintaining the correct cache value uses more CPU cycles than just recomputing": 100 bytes saved and 0.2% faster. fae58d51ce 2017-01-26 removed an obsolete last-rowid local from the VM loop. • dfcdc663f2 2017-09-01 Removed "an obsolete optimization in pcache" that "was recently making the code a little slower". • 02ceed058a 2018-08-03 and dda7e66d7d 2018-08-04 Removed the code generator's column cache: "the binary is almost 2KB smaller and the speed-check.sh performance test is over 3 million cycles faster".

### sqlite-090 — Make cache eviction aware of write cost and memory pressure
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite
Principle: When choosing a victim, prefer entries that are clean or cheap to drop. Start writing back before the cache is fully dirty, remember where the last search for a cheap victim ended, recycle existing entries instead of allocating under memory pressure, and bound the cache by bytes.
Why it is faster: Evicting a dirty page can force an fsync of the journal, which costs milliseconds. Keeping a pointer to the first clean, synced page makes the common eviction cheap. Purging early keeps eviction from stalling. Remembering the scan position avoids O(n^2) rescans of the same dirty entries. A byte bound stays right whatever the page size.
Applies elsewhere: Data / databases: Buffer pools that prefer clean victims and flush in the background (dirty-ratio thresholds). • Web / backend: CDN and object caches that evict cheap-to-refetch items first; byte-weighted LRU (Guava `maximumWeight`). • Native / systems: Clock hands and next-fit pointers that resume scans where they stopped. • Embedded: Memory-budgeted caches that recycle entries instead of allocating new ones.
Watch out: Preferring clean pages can keep dirty pages resident too long and turn the eventual flush into a burst; pair it with early write-back.
Seen in SQLite: 341eae8d35 2003-01-21 Keeps a pointer to the first synced page on the LRU list, which "makes a fetch of a page that is not in cache go a lot faster when the cache is full". • a858aa2e8c 2008-08-22 When recycling, tries "to find one that does not require a call to xSync() on the journal file". • 6fa0fe13f0 2009-03-05 Begins purging once 90% of the cache is dirty. 062d4cb0ae 2008-08-29 frees pages above the limit immediately instead of putting them on the LRU list, and d491e1bfd1 2008-08-26 recycles only clean pages from other caches. • 21b7ce6151 2008-07-07 Made the page list doubly linked for O(1) unlinking when releasing memory. • a963896796 2010-02-04 Stores where the search for a page that needs no sync ended, fixing "a performance glitch that appears for large transactions". • 50d1b5f363 2010-08-27 The page cache "tries to reuse existing pages, rather than create new pages, when SQLite is under memory pressure". 3b42abb35b 2011-11-09 lets a negative `cache_size` mean a size in KiB. • bc59ac0e26 2014-08-27 Split page fetch into Fetch, FetchStress and FetchFinish so the common hit is "significantly faster overall and about 100 bytes smaller".

### sqlite-091 — Store data in the form readers need: normalize once at write time
Lens: caching · Source: SQLite · Also: Seastar
Principle: Do conversions (case folding, binary encoding, sort order) once when data is stored or registered, rather than on every read or lookup.
Why it is faster: Reads usually outnumber writes. A conversion done at write time is paid once; the same conversion at read time is paid on every access. Storing data in the order queries want lets the cheap forward scan produce it.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Store canonical lower-case emails and usernames; precompute slugs. • Data / databases: Store precomputed collation sort keys; newest-first clustering for timelines. • Native / systems: Normalize symbol names at registration in plugin registries. • Embedded: Store sensor calibration already applied rather than per read.
Watch out: If you keep only the normalized form, the original is lost; keep both when display needs the original.
Seen in SQLite: b46ee91729 2011-06-04 FTS4 `order=DESC` stores doclists in descending order so the optimizations for ascending docid order also apply to descending queries. • ebaaa67db2 2017-07-06 Avoids case conversion when registering built-in functions. • 9201184711 2018-05-26 Stores application-defined function names in lower case "to avoid the need for case conversions before calling xFindFunction". • Also seen in Seastar (seastar-064): c262060d92 2014-11-12 memcached caches the preformatted `ascii_prefix` (flags and size) at item creation, avoiding `vprintf()` on every get: "Improves memaslap UDP posix throughput ... by 40% (from 73k to 105k)". • 838f79073d 2025-10-15 Metric label values are escaped once at ingress (`escaped_string`) instead of at every serialization. Key benchmarks ran about 2x faster: test_large_families went from 586.7 to 318.3 ns and from 11,010 to 5,485 instructions. • 71a972bdb3 2026-03-11 The HTTP `response_line()` is built once and written with one buffered write, instead of being assembled twice.

### sqlite-092 — Split hot and cold paths: tiny inline fast path, out-of-line slow path
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite · Also: Netty, Seastar
Principle: When a function usually finishes trivially, keep only that check in a small function and move the rare, heavy work into a separate function that is never inlined.
Why it is faster: Rare branches pull in locals, calls and callee-saved registers. The compiler then gives the whole function a stack frame, a prologue and an epilogue, and spills registers, even when the common path needs none of it. Moving the rare code out leaves a fast path of a few instructions that can be inlined into callers and stays dense in the instruction cache. The rare case pays one extra call, which is cheap compared with the work it does anyway. The same move also relieves register pressure in very large functions, such as a planner or interpreter loop, so the compiler optimizes the common code better.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: In JS, keep the hot function small and monomorphic and move error formatting or rare branches into a separate function, so the JIT inlines the fast path and does not deoptimize on rare input. • Native / systems: Mark growth, error and first-use paths `__attribute__((cold, noinline))` or Rust `#[cold] #[inline(never)]`. A buffered writer handles "fits in the buffer" inline and calls `flushAndWrite()` otherwise. • Embedded: On small cores with few registers, a prologue-free fast path saves stack traffic on every call.
Watch out: Only split when the fast path is truly dominant. Otherwise you add a call to the common case. Check the generated code, because the compiler may still merge the functions under LTO unless the slow path is explicitly marked noinline.

### sqlite-093 — Control inlining deliberately: force it where it pays, block it where it hurts
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite · Also: PostgreSQL, Seastar
Principle: Do not leave inlining entirely to compiler heuristics. Inline tiny hot helpers by hand or by attribute, shape call sites so the compiler can inline, and forbid inlining of large rare helpers that bloat a hot caller.
Why it is faster: Inlining removes the call, return and argument marshalling, and lets the compiler optimize across the old boundary: duplicate tests merge and constants propagate. Compilers usually inline only functions with one call site, no recursion and no indirection, and are conservative at -Os. Restructuring code so a hot callee has one call site, passing parameters instead of function pointers, or forcing inline, makes the profitable choice happen. In the other direction, inlining a big, rarely run helper into a hot function grows its frame and register pressure on every call. Partial inlining (inline only the common small cases, tail-call a helper for the rest) gives most of the speed for a fraction of the code growth.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: JIT inlining budgets are small. Keep call sites monomorphic and helpers short, and hand-inline the one helper in a tight loop that profiling shows is not being inlined. • Native / systems: Prefer generics over trait objects or function pointers in hot Rust or C++ code. Mark large fallbacks (charset conversion, bignum, decompression) noinline. Inspect per-function size after LTO. • Data / databases: Inline the record decoder into the interpreter's column-read handler, and keep the generic decoder for cold opcodes.
Watch out: Inlining everywhere bloats code and can make things slower through instruction-cache misses. Inline at the few hottest call sites, measure cycles and bytes together, and re-check after compiler upgrades.

### sqlite-094 — Order tests so the common case is decided first
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite
Principle: Arrange conditionals so the cheapest test that settles the most frequent case comes first, and return immediately on the hottest outcome.
Why it is faster: Every compare-and-branch on the way to the common outcome costs an instruction and a branch-predictor slot. Putting the frequent case first means the usual path executes one test. In short-circuit conjunctions, putting the cheap, usually-false term first skips the expensive term most of the time. An early return for the hottest status skips cleanup and bookkeeping code meant for the rare outcomes.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Test the cache hit or the 200 path before auth-error and edge-case handling; order route checks by traffic. • Native / systems: In a type switch inside a serializer, test string and number before rare types; in a queue, test the non-empty case first. • Data / databases: Order filter predicates by cost and selectivity, cheapest and most selective first.
Watch out: Frequency depends on the workload. Base the order on profiles of representative data, and remember that reordering is only free when the tests have no side effects.
Seen in SQLite: 56690b3d49 2012-09-17 `sqlite3VdbeSerialType()`: the file-format test was folded into the `u<=127` branch so the hot small-integer range is decided by the first comparison. 4261096124 reordered `c==matchAll && !prevEscape` in LIKE matching. • 7ea31ccb01 2014-09-18 `applyAffinity`: "Since numeric affinity is the most common case, check it first." e7ecdfabd2 makes `sqlite3MemCompare()` "try common cases first", and 1e98310a14 swaps two conditions for a 0.06% overall gain. • b34755308c 2015-06-29 `sqlite3PagerWrite()` returns at once for pages already writable and inside the file: "about a 1% performance increase". 6606586d81 then moved the error-code test behind that fast path, and e674bf1648 reordered a conditional. • 4d29448114 2019-10-05 OP_Transaction reorders an `&&` chain "for a small performance gain"; also 4011c44322. • 7e62146b13 2022-03-30 `sqlite3Step()` handles `SQLITE_ROW` first and returns before the end-of-statement code. 5cbb442ea3 and 57366d8cec test a flag only inside the branch that implies it. • 7a816e771a 2023-07-19 The JSON string scanner checks the common printable range first. f14b2e35fa turned the JSON value parser's if-chain into a `switch` on the first byte.

### sqlite-095 — Keep the critical path minimal: move rare work, checks and stores to the branches that need them
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite
Principle: Any computation, validation or store that only some branches need belongs inside those branches, not at the top of the function or loop body.
Why it is faster: A default value written up front and then overwritten on most paths is a wasted store. A length computed before a branch that rarely uses it is wasted arithmetic. A sanity check that only matters on a cache miss slows every hit. Moving the work removes instructions and memory traffic from the common path without changing results. A related trick: computing a value that a branch depends on before an unrelated store lets the CPU overlap them.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Build error messages and stack traces only on failure; compute content-length only when chunked encoding is off. • Native / systems: Validate keys only on the cache-miss path; avoid `result = default` followed by an almost-always overwrite in tight loops. • Data / databases: In a per-row decoder, keep the "row fits in one page" path free of overflow-only computations.
Watch out: Moving a check off the hit path is only safe if the hit path cannot reach the condition the check guards. Keep an assert on the fast path to document the invariant.
Seen in SQLite: e6f43fc4f8 2011-08-28 OP_Column: the unconditional `MemSetTypeFlag(pDest, MEM_Null)` moved onto the two branches that need it. ### sqlite-096 — Test the usually-false precondition at the call site
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite
Principle: If a callee usually returns immediately (no triggers, no listeners, NULL argument, feature disabled), put that one-flag test in front of the call so the call never happens in the common case.
Why it is faster: A call that does nothing still costs argument setup, the call and return, the callee's prologue, and often a cache miss on cold code. Testing a flag that is already in a register costs one compare. The trick is most effective when the callee is not inlinable or sits in another file, and when the "nothing to do" state is the norm.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: `if (listeners.length)` before building an event object; `if (logger.isDebugEnabled())` before formatting; skip middleware chains when no hooks are registered. • Native / systems: A `likely(!ptr)` test before calling `free_slow()`; check a "has work" bit before calling into a subsystem. • Data / databases: Check a "table has triggers" or "foreign keys on" flag before emitting or calling the trigger and constraint machinery.
Watch out: Duplicating the guard at many call sites costs code size and can drift out of sync with the callee. Wrap it in a macro or inline function next to the callee, and keep the callee correct when called without the guard.

### sqlite-097 — Keep hot values in locals and compute addresses once
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite
Principle: Copy struct fields, configuration values and repeated address expressions into local variables before a hot loop or long function, and write back once at the end.
Why it is faster: When a loop calls functions or writes through pointers, the compiler must assume `p->field` may have changed and reloads it each time. A local can live in a register for the whole loop. Macros that use an argument twice recompute its address expression; computing the pointer once removes that work. Loop-invariant lookups such as thread-local data, encoding or "is a callback installed?" belong in a local read once.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Cache `arr.length`, `this.config.x` and nested property lookups in locals inside hot JS loops; read a feature flag once per request, not per row. • Native / systems: Read `this->buf` and `this->len` into locals; hoist TLS and `errno` access out of loops. • Embedded: Shadow volatile-free state in locals so the compiler can keep it in registers.
Watch out: A cached local goes stale if the underlying field can legitimately change inside the loop; refresh it at exactly those points, as SQLite does after opcodes that switch programs.
Seen in SQLite: 8079a0d3f5 2006-01-12 The interpreter caches the database encoding in a local at entry. 70ff98ab74 avoided excess thread-specific-data lookups: "about 10%". 4b494d65a7 did the same in the parser: "about a 2.5% speed improvement". • bbe879d516 2009-11-14 "About 2% faster by storing the opcode array in a local variable", refreshed only where the program can change. a6c2ed91ca did the same for the register array and hoisted the progress-callback test out of the loop. • afb9817f8c 2011-06-04 B-tree binary search keeps the index in a local instead of re-reading `pCur->aiIdx[]`. c3f1d5f095 compares already-loaded flag locals instead of struct fields; 41692e9d7c shaved cycles in the page cache to beat the previous release's speed. • 009a48e0ad 2022-02-23 Precomputes `pTmp=&aData[...]` before each `get2byte()`: "almost a one-million cycle performance gain and a few bytes less code". Also 8ee75f7c3a, 16635fa777, 7f8f659260. • aec283385e 2026-02-21 Float decoding uses locals instead of structure elements; also 24ef9fbb19. 3bdebaeabb caches a table's column array and count: "Performance and size optimization".

### sqlite-098 — Specialize general routines per case; unswitch loops
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite · Also: PostgreSQL
Principle: When callers already know which case applies, give each case its own routine or loop instead of one routine that tests a mode, a type or a constant argument at run time.
Why it is faster: A shared routine re-tests its mode on every call, carries code for every variant, and needs registers for parameters that are constant for a given caller. A specialized copy has no mode branches, fewer live values and constant offsets, and often a single call site that the compiler inlines. Loop unswitching applies the same idea inside a function: test the invariant once and run a loop body written for that outcome. The cost is code size and the risk that two copies drift apart.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Split a JS function by argument shape so each copy stays monomorphic; prefer separate `copy()` and `move()` over `transfer(mode)`. • Native / systems: Templates or generics instantiated per type; image filters per pixel format; a separate loop for the "no nulls" case of a column kernel. • Data / databases: Separate insert paths for append-only keys; per-page-type decoders chosen once per page.
Watch out: Every clone is code that must be fixed twice. Specialize only measured hot paths, keep cross-reference comments in both copies, and stop when the size cost outweighs a cycle gain you can still measure.

### sqlite-099 — Run a cheap test before an expensive comparison
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite · Also: Netty
Principle: Before a string compare, case-folding compare or exact computation, test something that rejects most candidates for almost nothing: pointer identity, the first byte, a stored hash byte, or a conservative bound.
Why it is faster: Most comparisons fail, and most fail on the first difference. A one-byte hash, the first character or an equality of raw bytes settles the common case with one compare and calls the expensive routine only for likely matches. Pointer equality settles the case where callers pass the same interned object. A conservative bound that can only give false "maybe" answers lets the exact computation run only when needed.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Header-name maps that store a hash and compare it first; `===` before deep-equal. • Native / systems: Interned strings and symbols compared by pointer; length comparison before `memcmp`. • Data / databases: Column-name resolution in ORMs and dataframes; bounding-box tests before exact geometry.
Watch out: The pre-check must never give a false "no". Pointer identity is only a shortcut for "yes", unless interning is guaranteed, and a stored hash must be kept in sync with the key.

### sqlite-100 — Classify characters with lookup tables, and switch on the class
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite · Also: curl
Principle: Map each byte to a class or a set of property bits with one indexed load, instead of a chain of range comparisons or a locale-aware library call.
Why it is faster: One table load plus a mask replaces several compares and unpredictable branches, and one table can hold many properties as bits. libc `ctype` functions are locale-aware calls. A `switch` on raw characters compiles into a binary search over sparse values, while a switch on a dense class number compiles into a jump table. The cost is 128-256 bytes of read-only data.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: URL-encoding decisions, JSON and CSV scanners, template lexers; in JS, a `Uint8Array(256)` class table beats a regex per character. • Embedded: Locale-free classification that avoids pulling in libc ctype and its tables. • Native / systems: Base64 and hex decode tables, identifier tables in compilers.
Watch out: Index with an unsigned byte so negative `char` values do not read before the table. Generate the table from a script so it stays consistent with the grammar.

Where the trick could alias a non-letter (the digit in `pop3`), an explicit check is added.

### sqlite-101 — Replace branchy logic over a small domain with a constant table
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite
Principle: When a function's input is a small integer (a type code, an opcode offset, a serial type), precompute its outputs into a constant array and index it.
Why it is faster: A multi-way switch or if-chain costs several branches, and they mispredict when the input varies. A table read is one load with no branches and is usually smaller in code. Tables also combine well with consecutive enum numbering: index by `op - FIRST_OP`.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Map status codes or type tags to handlers or categories with an array. • Embedded: Popcount, CRC, bit-reverse and varint-length tables on MCUs without the instructions. • Native / systems: Opcode property tables in emulators and VMs; state-transition matrices in protocol parsers.
Watch out: Tables only win while they stay in L1 cache; a large table can be slower than a few predictable branches. Assert the enum ordering the table depends on.
Seen in SQLite: 1b27b8c0a0 2014-02-10 `sqlite3_value_type()` became a 32-entry table indexed by `memType&0x1f`: "Faster and smaller". The new planner's 8b76656ad4 adds two log-scale costs through a small difference table. • faf3727904 2015-10-16 `sqlite3SmallTypeSizes[]` extended to 128 entries so OP_Column looks up the length of one-byte serial types: 1.2%. • 5859636fb1 2017-08-03 Comparison opcodes: asserts that NE, EQ, GT, LE, LT, GE are consecutive, then `res2 = aLTb/aEQb/aGTb[pOp->opcode - OP_Ne]` replaces a six-way switch. 8546497383 uses the same tables in its integer fast path. • c2777abc2d 2022-10-11 OP_IsType uses a small constant mask table for serial types below 12: "slightly smaller and faster".

### sqlite-102 — Choose constant values so tests become one comparison and switches become dense
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite
Principle: Number enums, opcodes and tokens deliberately: put related values in contiguous ranges, start at zero, put the hot case at one end or at zero, and align values that map onto each other.
Why it is faster: "Is this a comparison opcode?" becomes one range test if comparisons are consecutive. A decision can become arithmetic on the value (a low bit, an offset). A `switch` over dense values starting at 0 compiles into a bounds check and an indexed jump with no subtraction; gaps force binary-search code. Making the common state equal 0 lets the compiler test it with the cheapest instruction. Giving two related enums the same numbers removes the translation switch between them.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Make "OK" status 0; group protocol message types so the parser dispatches through a dense table. • Native / systems: Order bytecode opcodes or AST node kinds so "is binary operator" is a range; start state-machine enums at 0 with hot states together. • Data / databases: Share numbering between token, AST and opcode enums so code generation copies values instead of translating them.
Watch out: Numbering becomes a hidden contract. Assert the required orderings (SQLite asserts consecutive opcodes) or generate the numbers with a script, or a later reordering silently breaks range tests.
Seen in SQLite: f2bc013c70 2004-10-04 "Save about 800 bytes of code space by aligning TK_ and OP_ constants", removing a 27-case switch; 9a43267ba2 did more. • 1f35012c11 2009-11-13 With OP_SeekLt..OP_SeekGt consecutive, `if(oc==OP_SeekGt||oc==OP_SeekLe)` became `UNPACKED_INCRKEY * (1 & (oc - OP_SeekLt))`: "Performance improvements and size reductions". • 8c8a8c4573 2013-08-06 An if-else chain in `resolveP2Values()` became a switch and the build script renumbered those opcodes to sit together: "More than double the speed". 66e810247e numbered OP_Next variants close together "for efficiency in switch() statements". • 7ea31ccb01 2014-09-18 Swapped two affinity codes so "has affinity" is `affinity>=SQLITE_AFF_TEXT`. • ed94af5eb9 2016-02-01 VDBE opcodes numbered from 0, which "obviates the lower-bound test on switch(opcode)". 7cc84c2cdd gives the smallest numbers to the opcodes `resolveP2Values()` switches on; ad5a9d715f renumbered printf constants from 0; 4ef07708df renumbered parser rules so the reduce switch's jump table shrinks. • 5c8241b875 2017-12-24 Parser actions renumbered so reduce actions come last, making `yyact >= YY_MIN_REDUCE` the first test. a8519d79b9 made `CURSOR_VALID` 0: "small size decrease and performance increase". 6a8700b97b, 13aa88d956 and ee6c5e5ae7 clustered token values. • 17c4865b83 2022-04-01 A 32-bit magic number became a `u8` state enum 0..3. daebb0f9a0 put `TK_IS` next to `TK_IN` for range tests. • fd6beda14b 2024-06-06 `TK_ISNOT` next to `TK_IS` "to generate faster switch() statements"; b458cbb601 and 48f5e52728 begin with `if(op>TK_GE) return 0; if(op>=TK_EQ) return 1;`.

### sqlite-103 — Use sentinels and always-valid objects so loops and fast paths need no extra tests
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite · Also: Seastar
Principle: Arrange data so the end of a search, the empty state or the disabled state fails a test the code already makes: a terminator element, a dummy object instead of NULL, or a size threshold of zero.
Why it is faster: A loop with a sentinel needs one compare per iteration instead of two (value and bound). An always-allocated array or a valid empty cursor removes NULL tests from hot loops. Setting a feature's threshold or range bounds to 0 when it is disabled lets the existing "is it small enough / is the pointer in range" test fail on its own, so the hot path needs no separate "enabled?" branch; the slow path sorts out which case happened.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: Return empty arrays or a no-op logger object instead of null; set a cache's max-entry size to 0 to disable it. • Native / systems: Sentinel head nodes in linked lists; `a[n]` sentinel in a linear search; NUL-terminated scanning. • Embedded: A rate-limit bucket with capacity 0 instead of an `enabled` flag.
Watch out: The sentinel must be impossible as real data and must be maintained on every mutation; a missed initialization becomes an out-of-bounds read. Keep a debug assert on the bound.

### sqlite-104 — Offer unchecked internal variants: non-NULL, pre-validated, internal API
Lens: cpu-microopt · Source: SQLite · Also: Netty
Principle: Keep defensive checks at the public boundary and give internal callers that already guarantee the precondition a variant without the check.
Why it is faster: A NULL test, an index validation or an "is the library initialized?" call costs a branch or a call every time, even though most internal callers can never fail it. A second entry point whose contract excludes the bad case (asserted in debug builds) drops the cost. The general version becomes a thin wrapper around the unchecked one, so there is no duplicated logic.
Applies elsewhere: Web / backend: A public validated `parse()` and an internal `parseUnchecked()`; validate a request once at the edge rather than in every handler. • Native / systems: `free_nonnull()`, references instead of pointers in C++, `get_unchecked` after a proof in Rust. • Data / databases: FFI bindings with checked and unchecked column accessors; hoist bounds checks out of per-row loops.
Watch out: The unchecked variant is only as safe as its callers. Assert the precondition in debug builds and keep the unchecked entry points private.
